Let's think of "expected ‘:’ or ‘)’ before string constant" in attempt0.c:134:11. Could that be due to the output section formatting in previous:

At line 134 column 11 maybe the second colon? Actually if GCC sees the string `"6:\n"` immediately followed by `:`, the C parser after parsing asm template string expects `)` or `:`? Wait in GNU C, the syntax is:
```
asm [volatile] ( stringliteral
                 : outputoperands
                 : inputoperands
                 : clobbers
                 : gotolabels );
```
If there are no output operands, you still need a colon before input operands. The first colon after string starts output list. In the code, there are two colons:
```
    :
    : [Amat] ...

Let's draft final asm carefully:

```
__asm__ __volatile__(
    "movw %[Amat], %%si\n\t"
    "movw %[Tmpv], %%di\n\t"
    "movw %[Yvec], %%bx\n\t"
    "movw %[Nc], %%cx\n\t"
    "testw %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 2f\n\t"
    "1:\n\t"
    "    fldz\n\t"
    "    fstpl (%%bx)\n\t"
    "    addw %[Dsz], %%bx\n\t"
    "    decw %%cx\n\t"
    "    jnz 1b\n\t"
    "2:\n\t"
    "movw %[Mc], %%dx\n\t"
    "testw %%dx, %%dx\n\t"
    "jz 6f\n\t"
    "3:\n\t"
    "    fldz\n\t"
    "    fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "    movw %[Nc], %%cx\n\t"
    "    testw %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "    jz 8f\n\t"
    "    movw %[Xvec], %%bx\n\t"
    "7:\n\t"
    "        fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "        fmull (%%bx)\n\t"
    "        fldl (%%di)\n\t"
    "        faddp\n\t"
    "        fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "        addw %[Dsz], %%si\n\t"
    "        addw %[Dsz], %%bx\n\t"
    "        decw %%cx\n\t"
    "        jnz 7b\n\t"
    "8:\n\t"
    "    movw %%dx, %%bx\n\t"
    "    movw %[Nv], %%ax\n\t"
    "    movw %[Dsz], %%cx\n\t"
    "    mulw %%cx\n\t"
    "    subw %%ax, %%si\n\t"
    "    movw %[Nc], %%cx\n\t"
    "    movw %%bx, %%dx\n\t"
    "    testw %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "    jz 0f\n\t"
    "    movw %[Yvec], %%bx\n\t"
    "9:\n\t"
    "        fldl (%%di)\n\t"
    "        fmull (%%si)\n\t"
    "        faddl (%%bx)\n\t"
    "        fstpl (%%bx)\n\t"
    "        addw %[Dsz], %%si\n\t"
    "        addw %[Dsz], %%bx\n\t"
    "        decw %%cx\n\t"
    "        jnz 9b\n\t"
    "0:\n\t"
    "    addw %[Dsz], %%di\n\t"
    "    decw %%dx\n\t"
    "    jnz 3b\n\t"
    "6:\n"
    :
    : [Amat] "m" (A),
      [Xvec] "m" (x),
      [Yvec] "m" (y),
      [Tmpv] "m" (tmp),
      [Nc] "n" (_PB_N),
      [Nv] "m" (n),
      [Mc] "n" (_PB_M),
      [Dsz] "n" (sizeof(DATA_TYPE))
    : "memory", "cc", "ax", "bx", "cx", "dx", "si", "di"
);
```

This is concerning. Let's examine with a small C example:
```
void f(int n, int a[n]) { asm("mov %0,%%eax" : : "m"(a)); }
int arr[10];
int main(){ f(10, arr); }
```
Inside f, parameter `a` is a pointer variable. If f is not inlined, `"m"(a)` refers to the stack slot holding the pointer; mov loads pointer value. If f is inlined, GCC must preserve the asm's semantics. Since the asm expects the pointer value at the memory operand location. The compiler can create a temporary stack slot holding `arr` value and use that as the memory operand. I think it does. It cannot simply replace `a` with `arr` because `a` in the asm input is not array-to-pointer decay? The expression `a` in the parameter is a pointer variable lvalue; to form a memory operand, it needs the pointer variable's storage. If inlined, it creates an SSA temporary, but for `"m"` it can assign it a stack slot. This is a known feature. So okay.

We want asm to implement source exactly even if n != _PB_N. Original loops:
- y[0.._PB_N-1] = 0.
- for i in 0.._PB_M-1:
   tmp[i] = 0
   for j in 0.._PB_N-1:
     tmp[i] += A[i][j] * x[j]   // A row i, j
   for j in 0.._PB_N-1:
